4886 BE7C D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
4887               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
4888               	; 			'
4889               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
4890               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
4891               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
4892               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
4893               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
4894               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
4895               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
4896               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
4897               	; 			'
4898               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
4899               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
4900               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
4901               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
4902               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
4903               	; 			'
4904               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
4905               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
4906               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
4907               	; 			'
4908               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
4909               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
4910               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
4911               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
4912               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
4913               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
4914               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
4915 BE7E D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     BE80 21BE     
4916 BE82 0201  20 	li r1,7936   ; 31*256
     BE84 1F00     
4917 BE86 0541  14 	inv r1

4919 BE8A C040  18 	mov r0,r1

     BE8E 21B0

     BE94 225C

5560               cv272
5561               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
5562               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
5563               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
5564               	; 		'
5565               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
5566               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
5567               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
5568               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
5569               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
5570               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
5571               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
5572               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
5573               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
5574               	; 		'
5575               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
5576               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
5577               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
5578               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
5579               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
5580               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1

6210               	;
6211               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
6212               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
6213               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
6214               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
6215               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
6216               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
6217               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
6218               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
6219               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
6220               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
6221               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
6222               	; 	'
6223               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
6224               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
6225               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
6226               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
6227               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

6787               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
6788               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is

6790               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.

6793               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
6794               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off

7616               	;
7617               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
7618               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
7619               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
7620               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
7621               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
7622               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
7623               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
7624               	; 	'
7625               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
7626               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
7627               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
7628               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
7629               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
7630               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
7631               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
7632               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
7633               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
7634               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
7635               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
7636               	; 	' flight looking busy.
7637               	; 	'
7638               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
7639               	; 	'
7640               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
7641               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
7642               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
7643               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
7644               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
7645               	; 	'
7646               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
7647               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
7648               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
7649               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.
7650               	; 	IF hrun > 0 THEN
7651 D344 D020  34 	movb @cvb_HRUN,r0
     D346 219C

     D34A 0100

8180 D77C C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8181 D77E 0450  20 	b *r0
8182               	;
8183               	; 	' ======================================================================
8184               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
8185               	; 	' ======================================================================
8186               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
8187               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
8188               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
8189               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
8190               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
8191               	; 	'
8192               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
8193               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
8194               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
8195               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
8196               	; 	' broken.
8197               	; 	'
8198               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
8199               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
8200               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
8201               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
8202               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
8203               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
8204               	; 	'
8205               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
8206               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
8207               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
8208               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
8209               	; coll_obst:
8210               cvb_COLL_OBST
8211               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
8212 D780 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     D782 2188

     D786 0300

8714               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
8715               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook

8719               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
8720               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
8721               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.

8784               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
8785               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
8786               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
8787               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
8788               	; 	'
8789               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
8790               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
8791               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
8792               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
8793               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
8794               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
8795               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

8920               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
8921               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.

8923               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
8924               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure

9612 E356 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
9613 E358 0450  20 	b *r0
9614               	;
9615               	; 	' ======================================================================
9616               	; 	' THE SCANNER
9617               	; 	' ======================================================================
9618               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
9619               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
9620               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
9621               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
9622               	; 	'
9623               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
9624               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
9625               	; 	'
9626               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
9627               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
9628               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
9629               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
9630               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
9631               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
9632               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
9633               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
9634               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
9635               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
9636               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
9637               	; esc_tick:
9638               cvb_ESC_TICK
9639               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
9640               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
9641               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
9642               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
9643               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
9644 E35A D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E35C 2176     
9645 E35E 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     E360 0100     
9646 E362 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
9647 E364 1402  14 	jhe cv753
9648 E366 0460  28 	b @cv752
     E368 E37E     
9649               cv753
9650               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
9651 E36A D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E36C 2176     
9652 E36E 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     E370 0700     
9653 E372 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
9654 E374 1A02  14 	jl cv755

     E378 E37E     
9656               cv755
9657 E37A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0

9659               cv754
9660               	; 	END IF
9661               cv752
9662               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
9663               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
9664               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
9665               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
9666               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
9667               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
9668               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
9669               	; 	'
9670               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
9671               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
9672               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
9673               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
9674               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
9675               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
9676               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
9677               	; 	escp = escp + 1
9678 E37E D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     E380 20B2     
9679 E382 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     E384 0100     
9680 E386 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     E388 20B2

9690               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
9691               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
9692               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
9693               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
9694               	; 	' 7 an east one.
9695               	; 	'
9696               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
9697               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
9698               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
9699               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
9700               	; 	'
9701               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
9702               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
9703               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
9704               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
9705               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
9706               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
9707               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
9708               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
9709               	; 	'
9710               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
9711               	; 	' move at all.

9966 E5D8 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
9967 E5DA 0450  20 	b *r0
9968               	;
9969               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
9970               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
9971               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
9972               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
9973               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
9974               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
9975               	; 	'
9976               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
9977               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
9978               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
9979               	; 	'
9980               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
9981               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
9982               	; 	'   +2  GREY
9983               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
9984               	; 	'
9985               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
9986               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
9987               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
9988               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
9989               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
9990               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
9991               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
9992               	; 	'
9993               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
9994               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
9995               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
9996               	; 	'
9997               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
9998               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
9999               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
10000               	; scan_furn:
10001               cvb_SCAN_FURN

10274               	; 	' THE FLIGHT IS BLACK, and it has to be written per character because the
10275               	; 	' colour table only knows rows -- the same reason the two actors carry

10277               	; 	'
10278               	; 	' This runs BEFORE the markers every tick, which is the whole ordering
10279               	; 	' the radar needs: the lift car is painted first and the flights next, so
10280               	; 	' a Kop or a crook standing on either of them paints over it. Nothing
10281               	; 	' moves aside to make room -- the pixels are simply ORed together and the
10282               	; 	' last colour written wins, so an actor in the lift shaft shows as an
10283               	; 	' actor rather than shifting anywhere.

10547               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
10548               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
10549               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
10550               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
10551               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
10552               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).

10626               	;
10627               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
10628               	; 	'
10629               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
10630               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
10631               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
10632               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
10633               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
10634               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
10635               	; 	'
10636               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
10637               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
10638               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
10639               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
10640               	; 	'
10641               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
10642               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
10643               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
10644               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
10645               	; 	GOSUB scan_elev
10646 EACA 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     EACC A980     
10647 EACE E838     	data cvb_SCAN_ELEV
10648               	;
10649               	; 	' -- Kelly. dotx 0..127 across the store, doty 0..23 down it; the canvas
10650               	; 	' is characters 8-23 of rows 21-23, so screen x is 64 + dotx.
10651               	; 	'
10652               	; 	' 167, NOT 168. The VDP puts a sprite's top line at y + 1, so a sprite
10653               	; 	' asked for the canvas's own row lands one pixel low -- which on a
10654               	; 	' three-pixel marker in a three-pixel band means its bottom row sits on
10655               	; 	' the yellow floor line. The character markers this replaced were poked
10656               	; 	' straight into the pattern table and needed no such bias, which is

11145               	; 	' falls through to the flash -- no GOTO, so the whole routine stays on
11146               	; 	' one traceable path

11312               	;
11313               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
11314               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
11315               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
11316               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
11317               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
11318               	; 	'
11319               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
11320               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
11321               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
11322               	; 	'
11323               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
11324               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
11325               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
11326               	; 	'
11327               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
11328               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).

11432               	;
11433               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
11434               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
11435               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
11436               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
11437               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
11438               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
11439               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
11440               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
11441               	; 	'
11442               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
11443               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
11444               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11445               	;
11446               	; do_escape:

11448               	; 	escapd = 0

     F138 2100

11580               	;
11581               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
11582               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
11583               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
11584               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
11585               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
11586               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
11587               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
11588               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
11589               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
11590               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
11591               	; 	' Harry was caught.
11592               	; snd_off:
11593               cvb_SND_OFF

     F268 8000

     F26C A980

11661               	;
11662               	; 	' ======================================================================
11663               	; 	' SOUND
11664               	; 	' ======================================================================
11665               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
11666               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
11667               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
11668               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
11669               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.
11670               	; sfx_tick:
11671               cvb_SFX_TICK

12019               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
12020               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
12021               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
12022               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
12023               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12024               	; 	'
12025               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
12026               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
12027               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
12028               	; 	'
12029               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
12030               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
12031               	; 	' letter after it.
12032               	;
12033               	; font_bits:
12034               cvb_FONT_BITS
12035               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space

12166               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
12167               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12168               	; 	'
12169               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
12170               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
12171               	; 	' being small sprites.
12172               	; 	'
12173               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
12174               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
12175               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
12176               	; 	'
12177               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
12178               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
12179               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12180               	;
12181               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
12182               cvb_SPR_KELLY

12903               	;
12904               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
12905               	; 	' Chars 160-207: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
12906               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
12907               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
12908               	;
12909               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
12910               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
12911               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
12912               	; 	' unreadable.
12913               	;
12914               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set
12915               cvb_SCAN_COL3
12916               	; 	DATA BYTE $EE,$EE,$EE,$EE,$EC,$EC,$EC,$BC
12917 6A9A EEEE     	byte >ee,>ee,>ee,>ee,>ec,>ec,>ec,>bc
     6A9C EEEE     
     6A9E ECEC     
     6AA0 ECBC

     6AA6 ECEC     
     6AA8 ECBC

     6AAE EEEE     
     6AB0 EEEE

13003               	; 	'   158 SLABP
13004               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
13005               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
13006               	; 	'
13007               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
13008               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
13009               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
13010               	; 	'
13011               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
13012               	; 	' blit is
13013               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
13014               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
13015               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
13016               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
13017               	; 	'
13018               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
13019               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
13020               	; 	'
13021               	; 	' template ids:
13022               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
13023               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
13024               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
13025               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
13026               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
13027               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
13028               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
13029               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
13030               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
13031               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
13032               	;
13033               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
13034               cvb_STOR_TPL
13035               	; 	DATA BYTE 149,149,149,149,105,149,149,149,149,149,149,105,149,149,149,149
